# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

154451 [main] INFO  du.ustb.sei.mde.mwe2.EcoreGenerator  - LLM Response:
{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@6b321262 (name: approve) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Approves a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. The operation validates that the application is currently in the PENDING state and that the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. On successful approval, the application's status is changed to APPROVAL and two information emails (one to the customer and one to the company) are generated and added to the application's email list. Returns true if the approval succeeds; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. If this.customer == null OR this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
  3. Set this.status = APPROVAL.
  4. Create an Email for the customer using Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and set its receiver to this.customer.email.
  5. Create an Email for the company using the same content factory and set its receiver to this.company.email.
  6. Add both emails to this.emails.
  7. Return true.
Related Features: Application.status, Application.amountOfMoney, Application.share, Application.emails, Application.company, Application.customer
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: boolean - true when the application is successfully transitioned from PENDING to APPROVAL and both emails are recorded; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The application exists, its current status is PENDING, the related Customer is not null, and the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO (canApplyForIPO == true).
Post-condition: When true is returned, the application's status is APPROVAL and two Email objects (one for the customer, one for the company) have been appended to the application's emails list. When false is returned, the application's status and email list remain unchanged., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@357bc488 (name: sendRejectionEmail) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Constructs and stores a single rejection email addressed to the customer of this application. The email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares purchased, and the amount of money paid. The constructed Email object is appended to the application's emails list.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.customer == null OR this.company == null, do nothing (return).
  2. Build the email content string by invoking Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney).
  3. Create a new Email instance with content set to the produced string and receiver set to this.customer.email.
  4. Append the new Email to this.emails.
Related Features: Application.emails, Application.customer, Application.company, Application.share, Application.amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Application::approve
Summary: Approves a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. The operation validates that the application is currently in the PENDING state and that the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. On successful approval, the application's status is changed to APPROVAL and two information emails (one to the customer and one to the company) are generated and added to the application's email list. Returns true if the approval succeeds; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. If this.customer == null OR this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
  3. Set this.status = APPROVAL.
  4. Create an Email for the customer using Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and set its receiver to this.customer.email.
  5. Create an Email for the company using the same content factory and set its receiver to this.company.email.
  6. Add both emails to this.emails.
  7. Return true.
Related Features: Application.status, Application.amountOfMoney, Application.share, Application.emails, Application.company, Application.customer
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: boolean - true when the application is successfully transitioned from PENDING to APPROVAL and both emails are recorded; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The application exists, its current status is PENDING, the related Customer is not null, and the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO (canApplyForIPO == true).
Post-condition: When true is returned, the application's status is APPROVAL and two Email objects (one for the customer, one for the company) have been appended to the application's emails list. When false is returned, the application's status and email list remain unchanged.

Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary: Constructs and stores a single rejection email addressed to the customer of this application. The email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares purchased, and the amount of money paid. The constructed Email object is appended to the application's emails list.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.customer == null OR this.company == null, do nothing (return).
  2. Build the email content string by invoking Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney).
  3. Create a new Email instance with content set to the produced string and receiver set to this.customer.email.
  4. Append the new Email to this.emails.
Related Features: Application.emails, Application.customer, Application.company, Application.share, Application.amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: void - no return value. As a side effect, a new Email is added to the application's emails list.
Pre-condition: The application has a non-null customer and company, and the customer has valid name, surname, email, and telephone fields.
Post-condition: A new Email describing the rejection (with full customer and transaction details) has been appended to the application's emails list.

Application::reject
Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. Only applications in the PENDING state can be rejected. On success, the application's status becomes REJECTED and a single rejection email is sent to the customer containing the customer's identifying information, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. Returns true on success; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. Set this.status = REJECTED.
  3. Call this.sendRejectionEmail() to create and store the rejection email.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Application.status, Application.emails, Application.customer, Application.company, Application.share, Application.amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: boolean - true when the application was successfully transitioned from PENDING to REJECTED and a rejection email was recorded; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The application exists and its current status is PENDING.
Post-condition: When true is returned, the application's status is REJECTED and a rejection Email addressed to the customer has been added to the application's emails list. When false is returned, the application's status and email list remain unchanged.

Application::cancel
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application. Only applications whose status is PENDING can be canceled; approved or rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true on successful cancellation and false otherwise. (Note: per requirement 5, cancellation is initiated by the customer via Customer.cancelApplication; this method performs the state transition on the Application itself.)
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. Set this.status to a CANCELED state (modeled here as remaining PENDING but flagged canceled; alternatively introduce a CANCELED enum value - assumed the system treats cancellation as removal/marking). For the purpose of this specification, mark the application as canceled by removing it from the customer's applications list and setting status to a terminal canceled state.
  3. Return true.
Related Features: Application.status, Application.customer
Related Classes: Customer, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: boolean - true when the pending application was successfully canceled; false when the application is not in PENDING state.
Pre-condition: The application exists and its current status is PENDING.
Post-condition: When true is returned, the application is no longer pending and cannot be approved or rejected. When false is returned, the application's status is unchanged.

  2. Build the email content string by invoking Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney).
  3. Create a new Email instance with content set to the produced string and receiver set to this.customer.email.
  4. Append the new Email to this.emails.
Related Features: Application.emails, Application.customer, Application.company, Application.share, Application.amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: void - no return value. As a side effect, a new Email is added to the application's emails list.
Pre-condition: The application has a non-null customer and company, and the customer has valid name, surname, email, and telephone fields.
Post-condition: A new Email describing the rejection (with full customer and transaction details) has been appended to the application's emails list.

Application::reject
Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. Only applications in the PENDING state can be rejected. On success, the application's status becomes REJECTED and a single rejection email is sent to the customer containing the customer's identifying information, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. Returns true on success; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. Set this.status = REJECTED.
  3. Call this.sendRejectionEmail() to create and store the rejection email.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: Application.status, Application.emails, Application.customer, Application.company, Application.share, Application.amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: boolean - true when the application was successfully transitioned from PENDING to REJECTED and a rejection email was recorded; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The application exists and its current status is PENDING.
Post-condition: When true is returned, the application's status is REJECTED and a rejection Email addressed to the customer has been added to the application's emails list. When false is returned, the application's status and email list remain unchanged.

Application::cancel
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application. Only applications whose status is PENDING can be canceled; approved or rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true on successful cancellation and false otherwise. (Note: per requirement 5, cancellation is initiated by the customer via Customer.cancelApplication; this method performs the state transition on the Application itself.)
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. Set this.status to a CANCELED state (modeled here as remaining PENDING but flagged canceled; alternatively introduce a CANCELED enum value - assumed the system treats cancellation as removal/marking). For the purpose of this specification, mark the application as canceled by removing it from the customer's applications list and setting status to a terminal canceled state.
  3. Return true.
Related Features: Application.status, Application.customer
Related Classes: Customer, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
Output: boolean - true when the pending application was successfully canceled; false when the application is not in PENDING state.
Pre-condition: The application exists and its current status is PENDING.
Post-condition: When true is returned, the application is no longer pending and cannot be approved or rejected. When false is returned, the application's status is unchanged.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Determines whether this customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO. Eligibility is based on the customer's canApplyForIPO flag, which is set to false when the customer has accumulated too many failed attempts, in which case the customer must contact the call center to regain access. Returns true when the customer is eligible; false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.canApplyForIPO.
Related Features: Customer.canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None.
Output: boolean - true when canApplyForIPO is true (customer may apply for IPO); false when canApplyForIPO is false (customer is restricted and must call the call center).
Pre-condition: The customer instance exists and its canApplyForIPO field has been initialized.
Post-condition: No state change; the operation is a pure read of the eligibility flag.

Customer::createApplication
Summary: Creates a new IPO application for this retail customer. The operation validates customer eligibility, validates the inputs (positive shares and amount, non-null document), and ensures that the customer does not already have an approved application for the same company. On success, a new Application is constructed, linked to the customer and company, and added to the customer's applications list with status PENDING. Returns true on success; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
  2. If company == null OR shares <= 0 OR amount <= 0 OR doc == null, return false.
  3. For each existing application in this.applications:
       a. If application.company != null AND application.company.name equals company.name AND application.status == APPROVAL, return false.
  4. Create a new Application instance with:
       - company = company
       - customer = this
       - share = shares
       - amountOfMoney = amount
       - allowance = doc
       - status = PENDING
       - emails = new empty List<Email>
  5. Append the new application to this.applications.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: Customer.applications, Customer.canApplyForIPO, Customer.email, Customer.name, Customer.surname, Customer.telephone
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
  - company : Company - the target company for the IPO application; must be non-null with a valid name and email.
  - shares : int - the number of shares the customer wishes to purchase; must be greater than 0.
  - amount : double - the payment amount for the application; must be greater than 0.
  - doc : Document - the legal allowance documentation uploaded by the customer; must be non-null with a non-empty name.
Output: boolean - true when the new application is successfully created and added to the customer's applications list; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO (canApplyForIPO == true and no failed-attempt restriction is active), the provided company is non-null, shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is non-null, and the customer has no existing APPROVAL application for the same company name.
Post-condition: When true is returned, a new PENDING Application has been added to this.applications, referencing the supplied company, share count, payment amount, and allowance document. When false is returned, this.applications is unchanged.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of the paid amounts (amountOfMoney) across all of this customer's IPO applications whose status is APPROVAL. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each application in this.applications:
       a. If application.status == APPROVAL, add application.amountOfMoney to total.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: Customer.applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None.
Output: double - the sum of amountOfMoney across all approved applications for this customer; 0.0 when no approved applications exist.
Pre-condition: The customer instance exists and its applications list is initialized (may be empty).
Post-condition: The customer's applications list is not modified; the returned double reflects the current total of approved payment amounts.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application belonging to this customer, identified by the target company name. The operation searches the customer's applications for a PENDING application whose company name matches the provided companyName and cancels it. Returns true on successful cancellation; false if no matching pending application is found or the matched application cannot be canceled.
Algorithm:
  1. For each application in this.applications:
       a. If application.company != null AND application.company.name equals companyName AND application.status == PENDING:
            i.   Call application.cancel().
            ii.  If the cancel call returns true, return true.
            iii. Otherwise, return false.
  2. If no matching pending application is found, return false.
Related Features: Customer.applications
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String - the name of the company whose pending application the customer wishes to cancel. Must be a non-null, non-empty string matching the name field of an existing Company associated with a PENDING Application owned by this customer.
Output: boolean - true when the matching pending application was successfully canceled; false when no matching pending application exists or cancellation fails.
Pre-condition: The customer is authenticated, and at least one Application exists in this.applications whose company name matches companyName and whose status is PENDING.
Post-condition: When true is returned, the matched application's status is no longer PENDING and it has been removed/marked as canceled. When false is returned, no application state is changed.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the total number of IPO applications belonging to this customer that have been completed - i.e., applications whose status is APPROVAL or REJECTED. Pending and canceled applications are excluded from the count. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. For each application in this.applications:
       a. If application.status == APPROVAL OR application.status == REJECTED, increment count.
  3. Return count.
Related Features: Customer.applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None.
Output: int - the number of the customer's applications whose status is APPROVAL or REJECTED; 0 when none exist.
Pre-condition: The customer instance exists and its applications list is initialized (may be empty).
Post-condition: The customer's applications list is not modified; the returned integer reflects the current number of completed (approved or rejected) applications.

6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Email::createEmailContent
Summary: Builds the textual content of an information or rejection email related to an IPO application. The content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares purchased, and the amount of money paid. This factory method is used both for the approval information emails (sent to customer and company) and for the rejection email (sent to the customer).
Algorithm:
  1. If company == null OR customer == null, return an empty string.
  2. Construct a content string using the following template:
       "Customer Name: " + customer.name + " " + customer.surname +
       ", Customer Email: " + customer.email +
       ", Customer Telephone: " + customer.telephone +
       ", Company Name: " + company.name +
       ", Number of Shares: " + shares +
       ", Amount Paid: " + amount
  3. Return the constructed content string.
Related Features: Email.content
Related Classes: Customer, Company
Input:
  - company : Company - the company associated with the IPO application; must be non-null with a valid name.
  - shares : int - the number of shares in the application; should be greater than 0.
  - customer : Customer - the customer associated with the IPO application; must be non-null with valid name, surname, email, and telephone.
  - amount : double - the amount of money paid in the application; should be greater than or equal to 0.
Output: String - the formatted email body containing all required customer, company, share, and payment information; empty string when required inputs are null.
Pre-condition: All non-null inputs are provided with valid field values; the caller intends to use the returned string as the body of an Email whose receiver is set separately.
Post-condition: No state is changed on any passed-in object; a new String value is returned to the caller.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

On success, a new Application is constructed, linked to the customer and company, and added to the customer's applications list with status PENDING. Returns true on success; otherwise false.
```java
   * Algorithm:
```
```java
   *   1. If this.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
   *   2. If company == null OR shares <= 0 OR amount <= 0 OR doc == null, return false.

   *   4. Create a new Application instance with:
   *        - company = company

   *        - allowance = doc
   *        - status = PENDING

   *   5. Append the new application to this.applications.
   *   6. Return true.

   *   - shares : int - the number of shares the customer wishes to purchase; must be greater than 0.
   *   - amount : double - the payment amount for the application; must be greater than 0.
   *   - doc : Document - the legal allowance documentation uploaded by the customer; must be non-null with a non-empty name.
   * Output: boolean - true when the new application is successfully created and added to the customer's applications list; false otherwise.
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

    }
    if (company == null || shares <= 0 || amount <= 0 || doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    for (Application application : this.getApplications())
    {
      if (application.getCompany() != null
          && company.getName() != null
          && company.getName().equals(application.getCompany().getName())
          && application.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    Application newApplication = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createApplication();
    newApplication.setCompany(company);
    newApplication.setCustomer(this);
    newApplication.setShare(shares);
    newApplication.setAmountOfMoney(amount);
    newApplication.setAllowance(doc);
    newApplication.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    newApplication.getEmails().clear();
    this.getApplications().add(newApplication);
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Returns the total number of IPO applications belonging to this customer that have been completed - i.e., applications whose status is APPROVAL or REJECTED. Pending and canceled applications are excluded from the count. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize count = 0.
   *   2. For each application in this.applications:
   *        a. If application.status == APPROVAL OR application.status == REJECTED, increment count.
   *   3. Return count.
   * Related Features: Customer.applications
   * Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None.
   * Output: int - the number of the customer's applications whose status is APPROVAL or REJECTED; 0 when none exist.

   * Post-condition: The customer's applications list is not modified; the returned integer reflects the current number of completed (approved or rejected) applications.

   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"

  public int getApplicationCount()

      if (application.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL
          || application.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.REJECTED)

        count++;
      }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->

      }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application belonging to this customer, identified by the target company name. The operation searches the customer's applications for a PENDING application whose company name matches the provided companyName and cancels it. Returns true on successful cancellation; false if no matching pending application is found or the matched application cannot be canceled.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. For each application in this.applications:
   *        a. If application.company != null AND application.company.name equals companyName AND application.status == PENDING:
   *             i.   Call application.cancel().
   *             ii.  If the cancel call returns true, return true.
   *             iii. Otherwise, return false.
   *   2. If no matching pending application is found, return false.
   * Related Features: Customer.applications
   * Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: companyName : String - the name of the company whose pending application the customer wishes to cancel. Must be a non-null, non-empty string matching the name field of an existing Company associated with a PENDING Application owned by this customer.
   * Output: boolean - true when the matching pending application was successfully canceled; false when no matching pending application exists or cancellation fails.
   * Pre-condition: The customer is authenticated.
   * Post-condition: When true is returned, the matched application is no longer pending (it has been removed from this.applications or its state has been updated so that it is no longer counted as a PENDING application). When false is returned, this.applications is unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancelApplication(final String companyName)
  {
    for (Application application : this.getApplications())
    {
      if (application.getCompany() != null
          && companyName != null
          && companyName.equals(application.getCompany().getName())
          && application.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.PENDING)
      {
        return application.cancel();
      }
    }
    return false;
  }

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. The operation validates that the application is currently in the PENDING state and that the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. On successful approval, the application's status is changed to APPROVAL and two information emails (one to the customer and one to the company) are generated and added to the application's email list. Returns true if the approval succeeds; otherwise false.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
   *   2. If this.customer == null OR this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
   *   3. Set this.status = APPROVAL.
   *   4. Create an Email for the customer using Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and set its receiver to this.customer.email.
   *   5. Create an Email for the company using the same content factory and set its receiver to this.company.email.
   *   6. Add both emails to this.emails.
   *   7. Return true.
   * Related Features: Application.status, Application.amountOfMoney, Application.share, Application.emails, Application.company, Application.customer
   * Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None (operates on the receiver application's internal state).
   * Output: boolean - true when the application is successfully transitioned from PENDING to APPROVAL and both emails are recorded; false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: The application exists, its current status is PENDING, the related Customer is not null, and the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO (canApplyForIPO == true).
   * Post-condition: When true is returned, the application's status is APPROVAL and two Email objects (one for the customer, one for the company) have been appended to the application's emails list. When false is returned, the application's status and email list remain unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean approve()
  {
    if (getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING)
    {
      return false;
    }
    Customer customer = getCustomer();
    if (customer == null || !customer.isEligibleForIPO())
    {
      return false;
    }
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);

    Company company = getCompany();

    Email emailContentFactory = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    String content = emailContentFactory.createEmailContent(company, getShare(), customer, getAmountOfMoney());

    Email customerEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setContent(content);
    customerEmail.setReceiver(customer.getEmail());
    getEmails().add(customerEmail);

    Email companyEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setContent(content);
    companyEmail.setReceiver(company.getEmail());
    getEmails().add(companyEmail);

    return true;
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

When validation passes, a new Application is created with the provided shares, payment amount, and allowance document, and is added to this customer's applications. Returns true on success and false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If shares <= 0, return false.
  2. If amount <= 0, return false.
  3. If doc == null, return false.

  5. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.company.name == company.name AND app.status == APPROVAL, return false.

       - company = company
       - customer = this
       - share = shares
       - amountOfMoney = amount
       - allowance = doc
       - status = PENDING
       - emails = empty list

  8. Return true.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company

    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}

    email : String
    name : String
}

    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Application::reject
Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. The operation is allowed only when the application is currently PENDING. On success, the application's status becomes REJECTED, a single rejection email is sent to the customer (delegated to sendRejectionEmail), and true is returned. If the application is not pending, the operation returns false.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. Set this.status = REJECTED.
  3. Invoke this.sendRejectionEmail() to create and store the rejection email for the customer.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: status, customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
Output: boolean — true when the rejection is recorded and the rejection email is created; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The application must exist and its status must be PENDING.
Post-condition: On success, the application's status is REJECTED and this.emails contains a new rejection Email addressed to the customer. On failure, the application's status and emails collection remain unchanged.

Application::approve
Summary: Approves a pending IPO application. The operation is only allowed when the application is currently in the PENDING state and the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. On success, the application's status is updated to APPROVAL, two information emails are generated and stored (one addressed to the customer and one to the company), and true is returned. If any precondition fails, the operation returns false without changing the application's status.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. If this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
  3. Set this.status = APPROVAL.
  4. Build the customer email content via Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and create an Email whose receiver is this.customer.email; add it to this.emails.
  5. Build the company email content via Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and create an Email whose receiver is this.company.email; add it to this.emails.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: status, amountOfMoney, share, customer, company, emails
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
Output: boolean — true when the application is successfully approved and the two information emails are created; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: The application must exist and its status must be PENDING. The associated customer must currently be eligible to apply for IPO (Customer.canApplyForIPO == true).
Post-condition: On success, the application's status is APPROVAL and this.emails contains two new Email entries (one for the customer, one for the company). On failure, the application's status and emails collection remain unchanged.

Application::cancel
Summary: Cancels this Application by transitioning its status to a canceled state. Used internally by Customer.cancelApplication when the matching pending application is found. Returns true when the cancellation is performed, and false when the application is not in a cancellable state.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
  2. Set this.status to a canceled state (modeled as REJECTED in the existing enum, since no CANCELLED value exists).
  3. Return true.
Related Features: status
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
Output: boolean — true when the application was PENDING and is now canceled; false when the application was already approved or rejected.
Pre-condition: The application instance must exist and its status must be PENDING.
Post-condition: On success, the application's status is no longer PENDING (it becomes REJECTED to represent cancellation). On failure, the application's status is unchanged.

Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary: Creates a rejection email addressed to the customer and appends it to this application's email collection. The email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares purchased, and the amount of money paid, as required by the rejection notification rule.
Algorithm:
  1. Build the email content string by calling Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney).
  2. Create a new Email with that content and receiver = this.customer.email.
  3. Append the new Email to this.emails.
Related Features: customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
Output: void — no return value. Side effect: a new Email entry is added to this.emails.
Pre-condition: The application instance must exist and its customer, company, share, and amountOfMoney fields must be valid (non-null customer/company, share > 0, amountOfMoney > 0).
Post-condition: this.emails contains an additional Email addressed to the customer whose content describes the rejected application.

If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

```text
Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Email::createEmailContent
Summary: Builds the textual content of an email related to an IPO application. The content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid. The same content format is reused for approval information emails (to the customer and to the company) and for rejection notification emails.
Algorithm:
  1. Construct a content string by concatenating the following fields in a readable layout:
       - "Customer Name: " + customer.name + " " + customer.surname
       - "Customer Email: " + customer.email
       - "Customer Telephone: " + customer.telephone
       - "Company Name: " + company.name
       - "Number of Shares: " + shares
       - "Amount Paid: " + amount
  2. Return the resulting string.
Related Features: content (the value being produced)
Related Classes: Customer, Company
Input:
  - company  : Company  — the company involved in the application; must be non-null.
  - shares   : int      — the number of shares purchased; must be greater than 0.
  - customer : Customer — the customer who applied; must be non-null.
  - amount   : double   — the amount of money paid; must be greater than 0.
Output: String — a non-null textual representation containing the customer's identifying information, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount, suitable for use as Email.content.
Pre-condition: All four parameters must be non-null; shares and amount must be positive.
Post-condition: A new String is produced; no objects are mutated.
