# Operation Specifications
Customer::createApplication
Summary: Creates a new IPO application for this customer targeting the given company. The operation validates the customer is eligible to apply for IPO and that the customer does not already have an approved application for the same company. When validation passes, a new Application is created with the provided shares, payment amount, and allowance document, and is added to this customer's applications. Returns true on success and false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If shares <= 0, return false.
  2. If amount <= 0, return false.
  3. If doc == null, return false.
  4. If this.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
  5. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.company.name == company.name AND app.status == APPROVAL, return false.
  6. Create a new Application instance with:
       - company = company
       - customer = this
       - share = shares
       - amountOfMoney = amount
       - allowance = doc
       - status = PENDING
       - emails = empty list
  7. Add the new application to this.applications.
  8. Return true.
Related Features: applications, canApplyForIPO (consulted via isEligibleForIPO)
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
  - company : Company — the target company for the IPO application; must be non-null.
  - shares  : int     — the number of shares to purchase; must be greater than 0.
  - amount  : double  — the payment amount for the application; must be greater than 0.
  - doc     : Document — the legal allowance documentation uploaded by the customer; must be non-null.
Output: boolean — true when a new PENDING application is created and added to this customer's applications; false when any validation fails.
Pre-condition: The customer must currently be eligible to apply for IPO (Customer.canApplyForIPO == true) and must not already have an APPROVAL application for the same company name. The provided shares, amount, and doc must satisfy the validity rules above.
Post-condition: On success, this.applications contains a new Application in PENDING status referencing the supplied company, shares, amount, and allowance document. On failure, this.applications is unchanged.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Indicates whether this customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO. A customer becomes ineligible after too many failed application attempts; to regain access the customer must call the call center. This operation exposes that eligibility flag for use by createApplication and Application.approve.
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.canApplyForIPO.
Related Features: canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean — true when the customer is eligible to apply for IPO; false when the customer is restricted from the application process.
Pre-condition: The customer instance must exist and its canApplyForIPO field must be initialized.
Post-condition: The customer's eligibility state is not modified by this read-only operation.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the number of IPO applications owned by this customer that have been completed, i.e., applications whose status is APPROVAL or REJECTED. Pending applications and canceled applications are excluded. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.status == APPROVAL OR app.status == REJECTED, count++.
  3. Return count.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int — the number of completed (approved or rejected) applications; 0 when there are none.
Pre-condition: The customer instance must exist; this.applications may be empty or null-safe.
Post-condition: The customer's applications collection is not modified.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications owned by this customer. Only applications with status APPROVAL contribute to the total; pending and rejected applications are excluded. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.status == APPROVAL, total += app.amountOfMoney.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double — the sum of amountOfMoney across all APPROVAL applications; 0.0 when there are no approved applications.
Pre-condition: The customer instance must exist; this.applications may be empty.
Post-condition: The customer's applications collection is not modified.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application owned by this customer for the given company name. The customer supplies the company name; the operation locates a PENDING application with that company and cancels it. Returns true when an application is found and successfully canceled; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.company.name == companyName AND app.status == PENDING:
            i.   Return app.cancel().
  2. Return false.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String — the name of the company whose pending application should be canceled; must be a non-null, non-empty string matching the company name on an existing application.
Output: boolean — true when a matching PENDING application is found and successfully canceled; false when no matching PENDING application exists or the cancellation cannot be performed.
Pre-condition: The customer must have at least one application whose company name equals companyName and whose status is PENDING.
Post-condition: On success, the matching application's status is no longer PENDING. On failure, no application is modified.

When validation passes, a new Application is created with the provided shares, payment amount, and allowance document, and is added to this customer's applications. Returns true on success and false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If shares <= 0, return false.
  2. If amount <= 0, return false.
  3. If doc == null, return false.
  4. If this.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
  5. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.company.name == company.name AND app.status == APPROVAL, return false.
  6. Create a new Application instance with:
       - company = company
       - customer = this
       - share = shares
       - amountOfMoney = amount
       - allowance = doc
       - status = PENDING
       - emails = empty list
  7. Add the new application to this.applications.
  8. Return true.
Related Features: applications, canApplyForIPO (consulted via isEligibleForIPO)
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
  - company : Company — the target company for the IPO application; must be non-null.
  - shares  : int     — the number of shares to purchase; must be greater than 0.
  - amount  : double  — the payment amount for the application; must be greater than 0.
  - doc     : Document — the legal allowance documentation uploaded by the customer; must be non-null.
Output: boolean — true when a new PENDING application is created and added to this customer's applications; false when any validation fails.
Pre-condition: The customer must currently be eligible to apply for IPO (Customer.canApplyForIPO == true) and must not already have an APPROVAL application for the same company name. The provided shares, amount, and doc must satisfy the validity rules above.
Post-condition: On success, this.applications contains a new Application in PENDING status referencing the supplied company, shares, amount, and allowance document. On failure, this.applications is unchanged.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Indicates whether this customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO. A customer becomes ineligible after too many failed application attempts; to regain access the customer must call the call center. This operation exposes that eligibility flag for use by createApplication and Application.approve.
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.canApplyForIPO.
Related Features: canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean — true when the customer is eligible to apply for IPO; false when the customer is restricted from the application process.
Pre-condition: The customer instance must exist and its canApplyForIPO field must be initialized.
Post-condition: The customer's eligibility state is not modified by this read-only operation.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the number of IPO applications owned by this customer that have been completed, i.e., applications whose status is APPROVAL or REJECTED. Pending applications and canceled applications are excluded. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.status == APPROVAL OR app.status == REJECTED, count++.
  3. Return count.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int — the number of completed (approved or rejected) applications; 0 when there are none.
Pre-condition: The customer instance must exist; this.applications may be empty or null-safe.
Post-condition: The customer's applications collection is not modified.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications owned by this customer. Only applications with status APPROVAL contribute to the total; pending and rejected applications are excluded. Returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.status == APPROVAL, total += app.amountOfMoney.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double — the sum of amountOfMoney across all APPROVAL applications; 0.0 when there are no approved applications.
Pre-condition: The customer instance must exist; this.applications may be empty.
Post-condition: The customer's applications collection is not modified.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application owned by this customer for the given company name. The customer supplies the company name; the operation locates a PENDING application with that company and cancels it. Returns true when an application is found and successfully canceled; otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  1. For each app in this.applications:
       a. If app.company.name == companyName AND app.status == PENDING:
            i.   Return app.cancel().
  2. Return false.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String — the name of the company whose pending application should be canceled; must be a non-null, non-empty string matching the company name on an existing application.
Output: boolean — true when a matching PENDING application is found and successfully canceled; false when no matching PENDING application exists or the cancellation cannot be performed.
Pre-condition: The customer must have at least one application whose company name equals companyName and whose status is PENDING.
Post-condition: On success, the matching application's status is no longer PENDING. On failure, no application is modified.

    StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
    sb.append("Customer Name: ").append(customer.getName()).append(" ").append(customer.getSurname()).append("\n");
    sb.append("Customer Email: ").append(customer.getEmail()).append("\n");
    sb.append("Customer Telephone: ").append(customer.getTelephone()).append("\n");
    sb.append("Company Name: ").append(company.getName()).append("\n");
    sb.append("Number of Shares: ").append(shares).append("\n");
    sb.append("Amount Paid: ").append(amount);
    return sb.toString();
  }

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.

  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public boolean createApplication(final Company company, final int shares, final double amount, final Document doc)
  {
    if (shares <= 0)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (amount <= 0)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (!isEligibleForIPO())
    {
      return false;
    }
    for (Application app : this.getApplications())
    {
      if (app.getCompany().getName() == company.getName() && app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    Application newApplication = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createApplication();
    newApplication.setCompany(company);
    newApplication.setCustomer(this);
    newApplication.setShare(shares);
    newApplication.setAmountOfMoney(amount);
    newApplication.setAllowance(doc);
    newApplication.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    EList<Email> emptyEmails = new BasicEList<Email>();
    newApplication.getEmails().clear();
    for (Email e : emptyEmails)

      newApplication.getEmails().add(e);
    }
    this.getApplications().add(newApplication);
    return true;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application. The operation is only allowed when the application is currently in the PENDING state and the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. On success, the application's status is updated to APPROVAL, two information emails are generated and stored (one addressed to the customer and one to the company), and true is returned. If any precondition fails, the operation returns false without changing the application's status.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
   *   2. If this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
   *   3. Set this.status = APPROVAL.
   *   4. Build the customer email content via Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and create an Email whose receiver is this.customer.email; add it to this.emails.
   *   5. Build the company email content via Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and create an Email whose receiver is this.company.email; add it to this.emails.
   *   6. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, amountOfMoney, share, customer, company, emails
   * Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
   * Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
   * Output: boolean — true when the application is successfully approved and the two information emails are created; false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: The application must exist and its status must be PENDING. The associated customer must currently be eligible to apply for IPO (Customer.canApplyForIPO == true).
   * Post-condition: On success, the application's status is APPROVAL and this.emails contains two new Email entries (one for the customer, one for the company). On failure, the application's status and emails collection remain unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean approve()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (!this.getCustomer().isEligibleForIPO())
    {
      return false;
    }
    this.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);

    String customerContent = Email.createEmailContent(this.getCompany(), this.getShare(), this.getCustomer(), this.getAmountOfMoney());
    Email customerEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setContent(customerContent);
    customerEmail.setReceiver(this.getCustomer().getEmail());
    this.getEmails().add(customerEmail);

    String companyContent = Email.createEmailContent(this.getCompany(), this.getShare(), this.getCustomer(), this.getAmountOfMoney());
    Email companyEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setContent(companyContent);
    companyEmail.setReceiver(this.getCompany().getEmail());
    this.getEmails().add(companyEmail);

    return true;
  }

   * Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. The operation is allowed only when the application is currently PENDING. On success, the application's status becomes REJECTED, a single rejection email is sent to the customer (delegated to sendRejectionEmail), and true is returned. If the application is not pending, the operation returns false.

   *   1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
   *   2. Set this.status = REJECTED.
   *   3. Invoke this.sendRejectionEmail() to create and store the rejection email for the customer.
   *   4. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails
   * Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
   * Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
   * Output: boolean — true when the rejection is recorded and the rejection email is created; false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: The application must exist and its status must be PENDING.
   * Post-condition: On success, the application's status is REJECTED and this.emails contains a new rejection Email addressed to the customer. On failure, the application's status and emails collection remain unchanged.

  public boolean reject()
  {

      return false;

    return true;

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application. The operation is only allowed when the application is currently in the PENDING state and the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. On success, the application's status is updated to APPROVAL, two information emails are generated and stored (one addressed to the customer and one to the company), and true is returned. If any precondition fails, the operation returns false without changing the application's status.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If this.status != PENDING, return false.
   *   2. If this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false, return false.
   *   3. Set this.status = APPROVAL.
   *   4. Build the customer email content via Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and create an Email whose receiver is this.customer.email; add it to this.emails.
   *   5. Build the company email content via Email.createEmailContent(this.company, this.share, this.customer, this.amountOfMoney) and create an Email whose receiver is this.company.email; add it to this.emails.
   *   6. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, amountOfMoney, share, customer, company, emails
   * Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
   * Input: None (operates on the receiver Application instance)
   * Output: boolean — true when the application is successfully approved and the two information emails are created; false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: The application must exist and its status must be PENDING. The associated customer must currently be eligible to apply for IPO (Customer.canApplyForIPO == true).
   * Post-condition: On success, the application's status is APPROVAL and this.emails contains two new Email entries (one for the customer, one for the company). On failure, the application's status and emails collection remain unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean approve()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (!this.getCustomer().isEligibleForIPO())
    {
      return false;
    }
    this.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);

    String customerContent = Email.createEmailContent(this.getCompany(), this.getShare(), this.getCustomer(), this.getAmountOfMoney());
    Email customerEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setContent(customerContent);
    customerEmail.setReceiver(this.getCustomer().getEmail());
    this.getEmails().add(customerEmail);

    String companyContent = Email.createEmailContent(this.getCompany(), this.getShare(), this.getCustomer(), this.getAmountOfMoney());
    Email companyEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setContent(companyContent);
    companyEmail.setReceiver(this.getCompany().getEmail());
    this.getEmails().add(companyEmail);

    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application on behalf of the bank. The operation is allowed only when the application is currently PENDING. On success, the application's status becomes REJECTED, a single rejection email is sent to the customer (delegated to sendRejectionEmail), and true is returned. If the application is not pending, the operation returns false.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"

      return false;
    }
    this.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.REJECTED);
    this.sendRejectionEmail();
    return true;
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void sendRejectionEmail()
  {
    Email email = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    String content = email.createEmailContent(this.getCompany(), this.getShare(), this.getCustomer(), this.getAmountOfMoney());
    email.setContent(content);
    email.setReceiver(this.getCustomer().getEmail());
    this.getEmails().add(email);
  }
